[rate-limit] client address taken from x-vercel-forwarded-for on a Vercel deployment. The limit IS applied to that address and the bucket is unchanged, but this build does NOT vouch for it: nothing has proved the edge overwrites an inbound copy of that header, so if it does not, a forged one puts the caller in a bucket of their choosing. Reported trusted:false until RESTORE-TRUST-R0.1.20: a deploy-time check that forges x-vercel-forwarded-for against the deployed origin and asserts the rate-limit bucket does not move, run where an operator sees the result. Because that is unproven, every caller on this address source also answers to the R0.1.24 attested ceiling of 20 scans per window, deployment-wide — so a forged header buys that many scans per window, not an unbounded number.
[rate-limit] the R0.1.24 attested deployment ceiling (20 scans per window, shared by every caller whose address came from x-vercel-forwarded-for — an assertion made outside the request that nothing in code can verify) is EXHAUSTED: scans on that address source are refused until the window rolls. R0.1.24: no trust basis is exempt from a ceiling. An operator-declared header and the Vercel platform header are attestations made outside the request that nothing in code can verify, so they answer to their own deployment-wide ceiling on a bucket no request header can move — far above honest traffic, finite, and separate from the unverified tier's. If honest traffic reached this, raise SCAN_RATE_LIMIT_ATTESTED_CEILING knowing what it buys. If it did not, that header is being FORGED and this ceiling is the only thing bounding the spend: RESTORE-TRUST-R0.1.20: a deploy-time check that forges x-vercel-forwarded-for against the deployed origin and asserts the rate-limit bucket does not move, run where an operator sees the result.

R0.1.22: where no client-address source exists that the caller cannot set, the per-address bucket is kept as a courtesy AND a deployment-wide ceiling is enforced on a bucket no request header can move. Rotating a header buys at most the ceiling, never an unbounded number of scans. Set SCAN_RATE_LIMIT_IP_HEADER to the single header your proxy OVERWRITES to get a real per-IP limit back, or raise SCAN_RATE_LIMIT_UNVERIFIED_CEILING knowing what it buys.
